308	## line
309	
310	### line.LineEnv: Continuous, several-agent test environment
311	`class LineEnv(Env): def __init__(self, points: int = 2, length: float = 5.0, max_steps: int = 50) -> None; name = "line"`
312	- **Invariants:** one side, `Side("point", 1, Controls(("push",), (-1.0,), (1.0,)), ids 0..points-1)`; each point starts at 0 and observes its own position / `length`; each step a point moves by its push, held at 0 from below; a point reaching `length` gets +1 and is removed (CORE-08), every other step gives 0; the game ends when every point is removed or after `max_steps`; score is the number of points that reached the end (CORE-07)
313	- **Errors:** as `core.Env`
314	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/line.LineEnv/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a game where point 0 reaches 5 first and point 1 two steps later
315	- **Used by:** registry, tests of CORE-07 and CORE-08; PL-01
316	
317	## chase
318	
319	### chase.ChaseEnv: Two-sided test environment
320	`class ChaseEnv(Env): def __init__(self, length: float = 5.0, max_steps: int = 20) -> None; name = "chase"`
321	- **Invariants:** sides `Side("chaser", 2, Controls(("push",), (-1.0,), (1.0,)), (0,))` and `Side("runner", 2, the same controls, (1,))`; each observes its own position / `length`, then the other's; the chaser starts at 0, the runner at `length`; each step both move by their push, held within `[0, length]`; when they are within 0.5 after a step the chaser gets +1, the runner 0 and the game ends; otherwise the runner gets +1 and the chaser 0; `max_steps` also ends it; scores are the chaser's touches (0 or 1) and the runner's steps untouched (CORE-10)
322	- **Errors:** as `core.Env`
323	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/chase.ChaseEnv/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a game where the chaser touches the runner on step 4; a replay double of a game where the runner lasts all 20 steps
324	- **Used by:** registry, tests of CORE-10; PL-01
325	
326	## agent
327	
328	### agent.AgentConfig: type
329	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class AgentConfig: memory: bool = True; frozen_copy: bool = True; eps_decay: bool = True; hidden: int = 256; lr: float = 1e-3; batch: int = 64; memory_size: int = 100_000; gamma: float = 0.9; refresh_every: int = 1_000; eps_start: float = 1.0; eps_end: float = 0.01; eps_fixed: float = 0.1`
330	- **Invariants:** the first 3 fields are the ablation switches (ABL-01–ABL-03); `eps_fixed` applies only when `eps_decay` is off; `0 < gamma < 1`
331	- **Boundary:** runs, train, study, registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agent.AgentConfig/ · **Cases:** the default (full agent); each switch off alone; all 3 off
332	- **Used by:** runs, train, study, registry
333	
334	### agent.DQNAgent: Hand-written DQN
335	```python
336	class DQNAgent(LearningAgent):
337	    def __init__(self, obs_size: int, n_actions: int, config: AgentConfig, *, seed: int) -> None: ...
338	    def values(self, obs: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray: ...
339	    @classmethod
340	    def load(cls, path: Path, side: Side) -> "DQNAgent": ...
341	```
342	- **Invariants:** `kind = "choices"`; `values` returns `n_actions` floats (AGENT-01); with `explore=False` it picks the highest value, ties going to the lowest index (AGENT-03); ε falls linearly from `eps_start` to `eps_end` over games `[0, n_games / 2)`, then stays at `eps_end` (AGENT-02); each `learn` remembers that id's step and makes one update, targets `r + gamma · max Q_frozen(s′)` or `r` when done, the frozen copy refreshed every `refresh_every` updates (AGENT-04, AGENT-05, TWIN-02); `end_game` returns `None`; memory off learns from the latest transition only (ABL-01), frozen copy off uses the current network (ABL-02); a loaded agent picks the same moves (AGENT-06)
343	- **Errors:** `load` raises `ValueError` naming both observation sizes and move counts if `side` doesn't match the file; `learn` raises `core.NonFiniteLossError` on a NaN or infinite loss or value
344	- **Boundary:** train, registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agent.DQNAgent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a recorded 3-game corridor training run; a saved full agent loaded back
345	- **Used by:** registry; PL-01, PL-04, PL-05
346	
347	## pg
348	
349	### pg.PGConfig: type
350	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class PGConfig: rung: Literal[1, 2, 3] = 3; value: bool = True; cap: bool = True; rescale: bool = True; hidden: int = 64; lr: float = 3e-4; gamma: float = 0.99; lam: float = 0.95; passes: int = 10; batch: int = 64; clip: float = 0.2; log_std_init: float = -0.5`
351	- **Invariants:** rung 1 is plain policy gradient, 2 actor-critic, 3 the limited-update agent (AD-13); `value`, `cap` and `rescale` are the tank's switches (ABL-06–ABL-08) and only rung 3 has them
352	- **Errors:** `ValueError` naming the switch and the rung if a switch is off on rung 1 or 2 (ABL edge case)
353	- **Boundary:** runs, study, registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/pg.PGConfig/ · **Cases:** each rung's default; rung 3 with each switch off alone; rung 1 with `cap` off (refused)
354	- **Used by:** runs, study, registry
355	
356	### pg.Spread: type
357	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Spread: centre: np.ndarray; width: np.ndarray`
358	- **Invariants:** one entry per control; `width` > 0 is the standard deviation of a normal draw around `centre` (PG-01)
359	- **Boundary:** pg tests · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/pg.Spread/ · **Cases:** an untrained rung's spread on a tank observation
360	- **Used by:** pg tests
361	
362	### pg.PGAgent: Hand-written policy-gradient ladder
363	```python
364	class PGAgent(LearningAgent):
365	    def __init__(self, obs_size: int, controls: Controls, config: PGConfig, *, seed: int) -> None: ...
366	    def policy(self, obs: np.ndarray) -> Spread: ...
367	    def value(self, obs: np.ndarray) -> float: ...
368	    @classmethod
369	    def load(cls, path: Path, side: Side, *, seed: int | None = None) -> "PGAgent": ...
370	```
371	- **Invariants:** `kind = "controls"`; one agent per side, learning only from the transitions it is handed, its own side's (PG-04, SELF-01); training moves are drawn from `policy(obs)`, evaluation moves are its centre, and both are clipped to the limits before they are sent (PG-01–PG-03); `learn` stores the step and returns `None`; `end_game` learns once from every stored step of every fish, then clears them (PG-04, PG-05), as AD-13 sets per rung and switch (PG-06–PG-09, ABL-06–ABL-08); a loaded agent picks the same moves (PG-12, SELF-07), and its training draws come from a generator built from `seed`, or from the saved run seed when `seed` is `None` (AD-22)
372	- **Errors:** `value` raises `ValueError` on a rung with no value estimate; `load` raises `ValueError` naming both observation sizes and controls if `side` doesn't match the file, another side's generation included (SELF edge case); `end_game` raises `core.NonFiniteLossError`
373	- **Boundary:** train, registry, selfplay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/pg.PGAgent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a recorded 3-game line run of each rung; a saved rung 3 loaded back; a saved predator generation loaded back; a prey generation loaded for the predator side (refused)
374	- **Used by:** registry; PL-01, PL-04, PL-05, PL-07
375	
376	### pg.draw: A training draw
377	`def draw(spread: Spread, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray`
378	- **Invariants:** one float64 entry per control, `centre + width · z` with `z` standard normal from `rng`; not clipped (PG-01); the same `rng` state gives the same draw (AD-18)
379	- **Used by:** `pg.PGAgent`; pg tests (TC-22)
380	
381	### pg.clip_move: A move clipped to the limits
382	`def clip_move(move: np.ndarray, controls: Controls) -> np.ndarray`
383	- **Invariants:** element-wise `min(max(move, low), high)`; a move already within the limits is returned unchanged (PG-03)
384	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `move`'s length isn't `len(controls.names)`
385	- **Used by:** `pg.PGAgent`; pg tests (TC-22)
386	
387	### pg.discounted_returns: One fish's returns
388	`def discounted_returns(rewards: Sequence[float], *, gamma: float = 0.99) -> np.ndarray`
389	- **Invariants:** one float64 entry per step, `G_t = r_t + gamma · G_{t+1}`, with nothing carried past the fish's last step, whether it was removed or reached the time limit (PG-05, PG-06, AD-13)
390	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `rewards` is empty
391	- **Used by:** `pg.step_weights`; pg tests (TC-24)
392	
393	### pg.advantages: One fish's blended advantages
394	`def advantages(rewards: Sequence[float], values: Sequence[float], *, gamma: float = 0.99, lam: float = 0.95) -> np.ndarray`
395	- **Invariants:** one float64 entry per step, the `lam`-blended sum of one-step errors `r_t + gamma · V_{t+1} − V_t`, with `V` after the fish's last step taken as 0 (PG-07, AD-13)
396	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the two lengths differ or are 0
397	- **Used by:** `pg.step_weights`; pg tests (TC-24)
398	
399	### pg.rescale: Advantages rescaled over one game
400	`def rescale(weights: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray`
401	- **Invariants:** `(weights − mean) / (std + 1e-8)`, population standard deviation over every entry given; the input isn't changed (PG-09)
402	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `weights` is empty
403	- **Used by:** `pg.step_weights`; pg tests (TC-24)
404	
405	### pg.step_weights: The weight of every step in one game
406	`def step_weights(rewards: Mapping[int, Sequence[float]], values: Mapping[int, Sequence[float]] | None, config: PGConfig) -> dict[int, np.ndarray]`
407	- **Invariants:** per fish, `discounted_returns` on rung 1 and on rung 3 with `value` off, `advantages` otherwise; on rungs 2 and 3 with `rescale` on, every fish's entries are rescaled together as one game (PG-06, PG-07, PG-09, ABL-06, ABL-08)
408	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `values` is `None` where the config needs an estimate, or its fish or lengths differ from `rewards`
409	- **Used by:** `pg.PGAgent`; pg tests (TC-24)
410	
411	### pg.capped_objective: Each move's objective
412	`def capped_objective(ratio: torch.Tensor, advantage: torch.Tensor, *, clip: float | None = 0.2) -> torch.Tensor`
413	- **Invariants:** element-wise `min(ratio · advantage, clamp(ratio, 1 − clip, 1 + clip) · advantage)`, or `ratio · advantage` with `clip=None` (cap off, ABL-07); not averaged; differentiable in `ratio` (PG-08, AD-13)
414	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the shapes differ or `clip` isn't in (0, 1)
415	- **Used by:** `pg.PGAgent`; pg tests (TC-25)
416	
417	## baselines
418	
419	### baselines.RandomAgent: Uniform random baseline
420	`class RandomAgent(Agent): def __init__(self, side: Side) -> None; name = "random"; kind: Literal["choices", "controls"]`
421	- **Invariants:** plays only `side`, the one it was built for (AD-25); `kind` is `"choices"` for `Choices` and `"controls"` for `Controls`; picks uniformly from `range(n)` for `Choices`, and each control uniformly within `side`'s limits for `Controls` (BASE-01), with a generator reseeded by `reset(seed)`, so a replay on the same seed is identical (REPLAY-04); ignores `obs` and `env`
422	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.RandomAgent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of 10 moves on Snake seed 42; a replay double of 10 moves in the tank on seed 2; a replay double of 10 predator moves on hunt seed 2
423	- **Used by:** registry; PL-01, PL-02, PL-05
424	
425	### baselines.ScriptedAgent: Greedy, danger-avoiding Snake baseline
426	`class ScriptedAgent(Agent): name = "scripted"; kind = "choices"`
427	- **Invariants:** reads `env.state`; steps toward the food along the axis with the larger gap, and if that move is immediately fatal takes a non-fatal move, preferring straight; if every move is fatal it goes straight (BASE-02); deterministic
428	- **Errors:** `TypeError` naming the environment if `env` isn't a `SnakeEnv`
429	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.ScriptedAgent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a Snake seed 42 game; food ahead with a wall ahead
430	- **Used by:** registry; PL-02, PL-05
431	
432	### baselines.ScriptedFish: Ball-fleeing tank baseline
433	`class ScriptedFish(Agent): name = "scripted"; kind = "controls"`
434	- **Invariants:** reads only the observation, in `tank.TankEnv`'s layout; always speed 0.3; with the wall within 1 unit ahead (forward wall reading ≤ 0.25), turns 15° toward the side whose rays read more room, left winning a tie; else away from the ray seeing the ball closest: rays 1–3 → −15°, rays 5–7 → +15°, ray 0 → toward the side with more room, ray 4 → straight; on equal ball readings the further-left ray counts; no ball in reach → straight (BASE-04); deterministic
435	- **Errors:** `TypeError` naming the environment if it isn't the continuous tank
436	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.ScriptedFish/ · **Cases:** the ball on the front-left ray only; no ball and the wall 0.5 ahead with more room on the left; the ball and the wall ahead at once
437	- **Used by:** registry; PL-02, PL-05
438	
439	### baselines.ScriptedPredator: Prey-chasing hunt baseline
440	`class ScriptedPredator(Agent): name = "scripted"; kind = "controls"`
441	- **Invariants:** reads only the observation, in `hunt.HuntEnv`'s predator layout; always speed 0.35; with a prey in reach, turns toward the ray seeing it closest: rays 1–3 → +10°, rays 5–7 → −10°, ray 0 → 0°, ray 4 → +10°; else with the wall within 1 unit ahead (forward wall reading ≤ 0.25), turns 10° toward the side whose rays read more room, left winning a tie; else straight (BASE-05); on equal prey readings the further-left ray counts; deterministic
442	- **Errors:** `TypeError` naming the environment and side if it isn't the hunt's predator
443	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.ScriptedPredator/ · **Cases:** a prey on the front-left ray only; no prey and the wall 0.5 ahead with more room on the right; equal prey readings on rays 1 and 7
444	- **Used by:** registry; PL-01, PL-02, PL-05
445	
446	### baselines.ScriptedPrey: Wall-, predator- and pellet-led hunt baseline
447	`class ScriptedPrey(Agent): name = "scripted"; kind = "controls"`
448	- **Invariants:** reads only the observation, in `hunt.HuntEnv`'s prey layout; always speed 0.3; first, with the wall within 1 unit ahead, turns 15° toward the side with more room, left winning a tie; else with the predator in reach, away from the ray seeing it closest, as `ScriptedFish` flees the ball; else with a pellet in reach, toward the ray seeing it closest: rays 1–3 → +15°, rays 5–7 → −15°, ray 0 → 0°, ray 4 → +15°; else straight (BASE-06); on equal readings the further-left ray counts; deterministic
449	- **Errors:** `TypeError` naming the environment and side if it isn't the hunt's prey
450	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.ScriptedPrey/ · **Cases:** the predator on the front-left ray only; no predator and a pellet on the right ray only; the predator and the wall ahead at once
451	- **Used by:** registry; PL-01, PL-02, PL-05

500	## selfplay
501	
502	### selfplay.SelfPlayConfig: type
503	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class SelfPlayConfig: rung: Literal["turns", "simultaneous", "selfplay"]; first: str; blocks: int = 10; both: float = 0.5`
504	- **Invariants:** `first` is the side that learns first when taking turns (the hunt: `prey`, SELF-03); `blocks` applies to `turns`; `both` is the share of self-play games played current against current, the rest split evenly between the two kinds against a past generation (SELF-05); `0 < both < 1`
505	- **Boundary:** runs, study, registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/selfplay.SelfPlayConfig/ · **Cases:** the hunt's `turns`, `simultaneous` and `selfplay` configs
506	- **Used by:** runs, study, registry
507	
508	### selfplay.make_schedule: The match for each training game
509	`def make_schedule(config: SelfPlayConfig, learners: Mapping[str, LearningAgent], n_games: int, *, seed: int, load: Callable[[Path, str, int], Agent]) -> Schedule`
510	- **Invariants:** `turns`: game g is in block `g · blocks // n_games`, block 0 learning `first` and the sides alternating, the other side playing its current learner frozen (SELF-03); `simultaneous`: every game both current, both learning (SELF-04); `selfplay`: current against current while the opponent has no generation, else a kind drawn with shares `both`, `(1 − both) / 2`, `(1 − both) / 2`, and the past generation uniformly from those added so far, from a generator built from `seed`; a past generation is `load(path, side, seed)`ed once, cached, plays frozen and never learns (SELF-05, AD-22)
511	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `learners` doesn't have exactly 2 sides, `first` isn't one of them, or `blocks` doesn't divide `n_games`
512	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/selfplay.make_schedule/ · **Cases:** a 20-game self-play schedule on seed 1 with generations added after games 2 and 4; a 20-game taking-turns schedule; 10,000 self-play matches after 4 prey generations, for the counts SELF-05 checks
513	- **Used by:** registry; PL-01, PL-04

606	## train
607	
608	### train.train_run: Train one run's learners
609	`def train_run(env: Env, learners: LearningAgent | Mapping[str, LearningAgent], config: RunConfig, *, opponents: Lineup = {}, schedule: Schedule | None = None, root: Path = Path("runs")) -> RunRecord`
610	- **Invariants:** checks `env` and the lineup first; plays `config.n_games` games on `training_seeds(config.seed, n_games)`, each game `schedule.match(game)`'s match, or every learner playing and learning when `schedule` is `None`; for learning sides `begin_game`, then each step `act(explore=True)` per living id in id order, `env.step`, `learn` per learning side's `Transition` in id order, and `end_game` after the game (AD-12, AD-20); every `eval_every` games records, per learning side, `evaluate` on the first `eval_games` held-out seeds against `opponents` for the other sides, and each side's mean loss; every `n_games / generations` games saves a generation of each learning side and passes it to `schedule.add_generation` (SELF-02); finishes with `final` over all `EVAL_SEEDS`, reusing the last point when it already is one, and saves each learner; same config → same record apart from `elapsed_s` (QR-05); works for any `Env` (CORE-05, CORE-07, CORE-10)
611	- **Errors:** `EnvCheckError`, `MoveKindError`, `MissingSideError` (a side with no learner, or, in a two-sided run, no evaluation opponent) and `RunExistsError` before any game; on any later failure, including `NonFiniteLossError` and `InvalidMoveError`, finishes the record as `failed`, naming the game and step, keeps its generations, then re-raises
612	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/train.train_run/ · **Cases:** a 200-game corridor run, seed 1; a run that hits a non-finite loss; a 100-game line run of rung 1; a 50-game chase run with both sides learning; a chase run whose schedule keeps the runner frozen
613	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-01, PL-04
614	
615	## registry
616	
617	### registry.make_env: Environment by name
618	`def make_env(name: str, **options: int) -> Env`
619	- **Invariants:** `"snake"` → `SnakeEnv()`, `"corridor"` → `CorridorEnv()`, `"tank"` → `TankEnv(fish=options.get("fish", 5), compass=bool(options.get("compass", 0)))`, `"hunt"` → `HuntEnv(prey=options.get("prey", 5))`, `"line"` → `LineEnv()`, `"chase"` → `ChaseEnv()`; the result passes `check_env`
620	- **Errors:** `KeyError` naming the known environments; `TypeError` naming an option the environment doesn't take
621	- **Boundary:** study, replay, crossplay, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/registry.make_env/ · **Cases:** `snake`; `corridor`; `tank` with 3 fish; `tank` with `compass=1`; `hunt` with 5 prey; `chase`; an unknown name
622	- **Used by:** study, replay, crossplay, cli; PL-01, PL-02, PL-04, PL-05, PL-07
623	
624	### registry.EvalSchedule: type
625	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class EvalSchedule: every: int; games: int`
626	- **Invariants:** `every` ≥ 1; 1 ≤ `games` ≤ 100; fills `RunConfig.eval_every` and `RunConfig.eval_games` (AD-15)
627	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/registry.EvalSchedule/ · **Cases:** Snake's `(50, 100)`; the tank's `(100, 20)` for 1,000 games; the tank's `(1, 20)` for 5 games
628	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-01, PL-04
629	
630	### registry.eval_schedule: Evaluation cadence per environment
631	`def eval_schedule(env_name: str, n_games: int) -> EvalSchedule`
632	- **Invariants:** `EvalSchedule(50, 100)` for `snake` and `corridor`; `EvalSchedule(max(1, n_games // 10), 20)` for `tank`, `hunt`, `line` and `chase` (EVAL-02, AD-15)
633	- **Errors:** `KeyError` naming the known environments
634	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/registry.eval_schedule/ · **Cases:** snake with 1,000 games; tank with 1,000 games; tank with 5 games; hunt with 1,000 games
635	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-01, PL-04
636	
637	### registry.make_agent: One side's learner for a run
638	`def make_agent(config: RunConfig, env: Env, side: str) -> LearningAgent`
639	- **Invariants:** from `config.agents[side]` and `env`'s side of that name: `"dqn"` → `DQNAgent(side.obs_size, side.moves.n, spec.config, seed=config.seed)`; `"pg"` → `PGAgent(side.obs_size, side.moves, spec.config, seed=config.seed)`, so the predator and the prey each get their own (SELF-01); the result passes `check_agent` for that side
640	- **Errors:** `MoveKindError` naming both kinds (the DQN on the continuous tank, a rung on Snake); `ValueError` for a baseline kind or a name that isn't a side
641	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/registry.make_agent/ · **Cases:** the full DQN on Snake; rung 3 on the tank; the DQN on the compass tank; the DQN on the continuous tank (refused); rung 3 for the hunt's predator
642	- **Used by:** registry.make_run, study, cli; PL-01, PL-04
643	
644	### registry.RunSetup: type
645	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class RunSetup: env: Env; learners: dict[str, LearningAgent]; opponents: Lineup; schedule: Schedule | None`
646	- **Invariants:** `learners` has one learner per learning side of the config; `opponents` holds, for a two-sided environment, each side's scripted agent, or a `RandomAgent` built for the side where it has none (the chase, AD-25), and is empty for a one-sided one; `schedule` is set exactly when `config.selfplay` is (AD-20)
647	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/registry.RunSetup/ · **Cases:** the full DQN on Snake; a hunt self-play run with both learners, both scripted opponents and its schedule; a chase run
648	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-01, PL-04
649	
650	### registry.make_run: Everything a training run needs
651	`def make_run(config: RunConfig) -> RunSetup`
652	- **Invariants:** `make_env(config.env, **dict(config.env_options))`, `make_agent` for each learning side, the opponents, and with `config.selfplay` `selfplay.make_schedule(config.selfplay, learners, config.n_games, seed=config.seed, load=…)`, the loader loading a generation with `load_agent`'s rules (AD-22)
653	- **Errors:** as `make_env` and `make_agent`
654	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/registry.make_run/ · **Cases:** the full Snake agent; a hunt self-play run on seed 2; a hunt simultaneous run
655	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-01, PL-04
656	
657	### registry.load_agent: Agent by name, run folder or generation
658	`def load_agent(spec: str, env: Env, side: str | None = None) -> Agent`
659	- **Invariants:** for `side`, or the only side when `None`: `"random"` gives `RandomAgent(side)` for that side (AD-25); `"scripted"` gives `ScriptedAgent` on Snake, `ScriptedFish` on the continuous tank, and `ScriptedPredator` or `ScriptedPrey` on the hunt's sides (AD-08); a run folder gives that side's final agent and `<run folder>@<n>` its generation n, loaded with `DQNAgent.load` or `PGAgent.load` as the record's `AgentSpec` says (SELF-07, REPLAY-01)
660	- **Errors:** `FileNotFoundError` naming the run or generation if it doesn't exist or has no saved agent; `ValueError` naming the environment and side if `"scripted"` has none there, or if `side` is `None` on a two-sided environment
661	- **Boundary:** replay, study, crossplay, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/registry.load_agent/ · **Cases:** `random`; `scripted` on snake; `scripted` on the tank; `scripted` for the hunt's prey; a saved DQN run folder; a saved rung 3 run folder; `runs/hunt/selfplay-s1@10` for the predator; a missing run
662	- **Used by:** replay, study, crossplay, cli; PL-01, PL-02, PL-04, PL-05, PL-07
663	
664	## curves
665	
666	### curves.CurvePlot: type
667	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class CurvePlot: path: Path; lines: tuple[str, ...]; skipped: tuple[str, ...]`
668	- **Invariants:** `lines` are the drawn labels, baselines included; `skipped` names every record with no evaluation points
669	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/curves.CurvePlot/ · **Cases:** the full agent with 3 ablations and 2 baselines; one record skipped; the 3 rungs with 2 baselines; the predator's 3 self-play rungs with 2 baselines
670	- **Used by:** study, cli
671	
672	### curves.plot_curves: Learning-curve chart
673	`def plot_curves(records: Sequence[RunRecord], out: Path, *, baselines: Sequence[RunRecord] = (), side: str | None = None) -> CurvePlot`
674	- **Invariants:** draws `side`'s evaluation results, or the only side's when `None`; one line per config minus seed: the mean evaluation score at each evaluation point, with a min–max band across seeds (CURVE-01, CURVE-02); each baseline record whose other sides all play `scripted` as a labelled flat line of `side`'s final mean (CURVE-03); labels name the agent and each switch that is off: `full` or the DQN's switches off (CURVE-04), a rung's name (`reinforce`, `actor-critic`, `ppo`) plus any switch off (CURVE-06), a self-play rung's name (`turns`, `simultaneous`, `selfplay`, CURVE-07), `compass` added for the compass tank; reads only records, in ≤ 10 s (CURVE-05)
675	- **Errors:** `ValueError` naming the environments if the records come from more than one, or naming the sides if `side` is `None` for a two-sided environment or isn't one of its sides
676	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/curves.plot_curves/ · **Cases:** the full agent, 3 ablations and 2 baselines; 5 seeds of each rung and 2 tank baselines; 5 seeds of each self-play rung for the prey with the 3 hunt baseline records; records from two environments
677	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-03, PL-04
678	
679	## replay
680	
681	### replay.Replay: type
682	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Replay: agents: dict[str, str]; env: str; seed: int; frames: tuple[Frame, ...]; scores: Scores`
683	- **Invariants:** `agents` is the spec each side was loaded from; `frames` runs from the first frame to game over; `scores == frames[-1].scores`
684	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/replay.Replay/ · **Cases:** the scripted agent on Snake seed 42; the random agent on corridor seed 3; the scripted fish on tank seed 2; predator generation 10 against prey generation 3 on hunt seed 42
685	- **Used by:** cli
686	
687	### replay.AgentSpecs: type
688	`AgentSpecs: TypeAlias = str | Mapping[str, str]`
689	- **Invariants:** a lone agent spec on a one-sided environment, or one spec per side name; a spec is `random`, `scripted`, a run folder, or `<run>@<n>` for its generation n (REPLAY-01, SELF-07)
690	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/replay.AgentSpecs/ · **Cases:** `scripted` alone; `{"predator": "runs/hunt/selfplay-s1@10", "prey": "runs/hunt/selfplay-s1@3"}`
691	- **Used by:** cli; PL-05
692	
693	### replay.record_replay: Re-play a game
694	`def record_replay(agents: AgentSpecs, env_name: str, seed: int, **options: int) -> Replay`
695	- **Invariants:** a lone spec on a one-sided environment, or one per side (REPLAY-01); `make_env(env_name, **options)`, taking the first run folder's own `env_options` when none are given, then `load_agent` per side and `play_game(..., explore=False, frames=True)`; the same inputs give identical frames (REPLAY-04); on a held-out seed a side's score equals its logged evaluation score against the same opponent (REPLAY-05)
696	- **Errors:** as `registry.load_agent` and `registry.make_env`, and `MissingSideError` for a side with no spec, raised before any window opens
697	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/replay.record_replay/ · **Cases:** the scripted agent on Snake seed 42; a saved rung on tank seed 1,000,000; predator generation 10 against prey generation 3 on hunt seed 42
698	- **Used by:** cli; PL-05
699	
700	### replay.Playback: type
701	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Playback: frames: tuple[int, ...]; fps: tuple[int, ...]`
702	- **Invariants:** one entry per frame shown, in order: `frames[i]` indexes `Replay.frames` and `fps[i]` is the speed when it was shown, within 1–30; the two have the same length; a pause adds no entry, and a step while paused adds exactly the next index (REPLAY-03)
703	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/replay.Playback/ · **Cases:** a 3-frame replay played through at 10 fps; paused after frame 0 then stepped once; sped up to 30 fps after frame 1
704	- **Used by:** cli
705	
706	### replay.show: Replay window
707	`def show(replay: Replay, *, fps: int = 10) -> Playback`
708	- **Invariants:** draws each frame's cells, then its shapes, with the step, each side's score and the caption (REPLAY-02); space pauses, → steps exactly one frame while paused, ↑/↓ change the speed within 1–30 fps (REPLAY-03); stays on the last frame until closed, then returns what it showed; reads keys only from the pygame event queue, so a test drives it headless with SDL's dummy video driver and posted key events
709	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `fps` is outside 1–30
710	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/replay.show/ · **Cases:** a 3-frame corridor replay shown headless; a 3-frame tank replay shown headless; a 3-frame hunt replay shown headless
711	- **Used by:** cli; PL-05
712	
713	## crossplay
714	
715	### crossplay.CrossTable: type
716	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class CrossTable: run: str; rows: str; cols: str; row_gens: tuple[int, ...]; col_gens: tuple[int, ...]; seeds: tuple[int, ...]; scores: dict[str, np.ndarray]; complete: bool`
717	- **Invariants:** `rows` is the predator side and `cols` the prey side (CROSS-02); `seeds` are the first 20 `EVAL_SEEDS`; `scores[side]` is float64 shaped `(len(row_gens), len(col_gens), len(seeds))`, that side's score in each game; `complete` exactly when both sides have 10 generations (CROSS edge case)
718	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/crossplay.CrossTable/ · **Cases:** a full 10 × 10 table; a table from a run with 6 generations per side (incomplete)
719	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-04, PL-07
720	
721	### crossplay.build_table: Every generation against every other
722	`def build_table(run: Path, *, games: int = 20) -> CrossTable`
723	- **Invariants:** reads the run's record, loads every saved generation with `registry.load_agent(f"{run}@{n}", env, side)`, plays each row generation against each column generation on the first `games` held-out seeds with `play_game(..., explore=False)` (CROSS-01), and writes `run/crossplay.json`; never trains (CROSS-02, AD-23)
724	- **Errors:** `FileNotFoundError` naming the run; `ValueError` naming the run if its environment hasn't two sides or a side has no generation
725	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/crossplay.build_table/ · **Cases:** a finished 20-game chase run with 2 generations per side; a run with no generations (refused)
726	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-04, PL-07
727	
728	### crossplay.load_table: Read a saved table
729	`def load_table(path: Path) -> CrossTable`
730	- **Errors:** `FileNotFoundError` naming the run if `crossplay.json` is missing
731	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/crossplay.load_table/ · **Cases:** a saved 10 × 10 table
732	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-04, PL-07
733	
734	### crossplay.TablePage: type
735	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class TablePage: path: Path; title: str; runs: tuple[str, ...]; incomplete: tuple[str, ...]`
736	- **Invariants:** `path` is the Markdown file written; `runs` names every table's run in input order; `incomplete` names those whose table isn't `complete`, in the same order, and is exactly the list printed under the table (CROSS edge case)
737	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/crossplay.TablePage/ · **Cases:** a rung's page over 5 full tables; a page over 4 full tables and one incomplete
738	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-04, PL-07
739	
740	### crossplay.write_table: One rung's table for the notes
741	`def write_table(tables: Sequence[CrossTable], out: Path, *, title: str) -> TablePage`
742	- **Invariants:** writes one Markdown table to `out`: a row per predator generation, a column per prey generation, each cell `predator mean / prey mean` over every table's games (CROSS-02); a generation missing from a table is averaged over the tables that have it, and the incomplete runs are listed under it and returned in `incomplete`
743	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `tables` is empty or mixes environments
744	- **Boundary:** study, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/crossplay.write_table/ · **Cases:** 5 full tables of one rung; 4 full tables and one incomplete
745	- **Used by:** study, cli; PL-04, PL-07
746	
747	## study
748	
749	### study.Variant: type
750	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Variant: agents: dict[str, AgentSpec]; selfplay: SelfPlayConfig | None = None; generations: int = 0`
751	- **Invariants:** one `AgentSpec` per side of the plan's environment; fills `RunConfig`'s fields of the same names
752	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/study.Variant/ · **Cases:** Snake's `no-memory`; the tank's `ppo`; the hunt's `selfplay`
753	- **Used by:** study.StudyPlan
754	
755	### study.StudyPlan: type
756	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class StudyPlan: env: str; full: str; learners: tuple[str, ...]; ablations: tuple[str, ...]; configs: dict[str, Variant]; env_options: tuple[tuple[str, int], ...]; sides: tuple[str, ...]; ladder: bool; optional: tuple[str, ...]; crossplay: bool; sections: tuple[str, ...]`
757	- **Invariants:** `configs` has one entry per variant name; `full` is in `learners`, the agents compared with both baselines, in rung order; `ladder` compares each learner with the one before; `ablations` are compared with `full`; `optional` learners may be skipped (SELF-06); `crossplay` builds each run's table and compares each side's last generation with its first; `sections` are the notes headings required (AD-16)
758	- **Invariants (plans):** `STUDIES["snake"]`: `full` / (`full`,) / (`no-memory`, `no-frozen`, `no-decay`), sides (`snake`,), no ladder; `STUDIES["tank"]`: `ppo` / (`reinforce`, `actor-critic`, `ppo`) / (`no-value`, `no-cap`, `no-rescale`), 5 fish, sides (`fish`,), a ladder; `STUDIES["hunt"]`: `selfplay` / (`turns`, `simultaneous`, `selfplay`) / (), both sides rung 3 with 10 generations, 5 prey, sides (`predator`, `prey`), `turns` optional, cross-play, sections "Why it learns", "Why self-play works" and one per rung (AD-24)
759	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/study.StudyPlan/ · **Cases:** `STUDIES["snake"]`; `STUDIES["tank"]`; `STUDIES["hunt"]`
760	- **Used by:** study.run_study, study.check_notes; PL-04, PL-06
761	
762	### study.Comparison: type
763	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Comparison: label: str; verdict: Literal["beats", "does not beat", "better", "hurts learning", "later beats earlier", "no detectable difference", "missing"]; method: str; statistic: float | None; p_value: float | None; p_adjusted: float | None`
764	- **Invariants:** baseline comparisons use "beats", "does not beat" or "missing" (EVAL-03); ladder comparisons "better", "no detectable difference" or "missing" (PG-10); ablation comparisons "hurts learning", "no detectable difference" or "missing" (ABL-04); generation comparisons "later beats earlier" or "no detectable difference" (CROSS-03); "missing" has no statistics; in a two-sided environment `label` names the side
765	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/study.Comparison/ · **Cases:** the agent beats the random baseline; an ablation with no detectable difference; a missing ablation; rung 3 better than rung 2; the self-play prey's later generation beats its earlier
766	- **Used by:** cli
767	
768	### study.compare_scores: One-sided comparison (null-hypothesis-testing)
769	`def compare_scores(full: np.ndarray, other: np.ndarray, *, label: str, n_comparisons: int, alpha: float = 0.05) -> Comparison`
770	- **Invariants:** `full` and `other` are each k × 100 (finished training seed × held-out seed, 1 ≤ k ≤ 5; a baseline is 1 × 100), columns in `EVAL_SEEDS` order; pairs by held-out game on each side's per-game mean over its rows; tests whether `full` is higher, corrected for `n_comparisons`; `method` states the seeds each side used (AD-09); the test is built with the **`null-hypothesis-testing`** skill
771	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the column counts differ, or if either side has no rows or more than 5
772	- **Used by:** study.run_study; PL-04
773	
774	### study.compare_generations: Last generation against first (null-hypothesis-testing)
775	`def compare_generations(last: np.ndarray, first: np.ndarray, *, label: str, n_comparisons: int, alpha: float = 0.05) -> Comparison`
776	- **Invariants:** `last` and `first` are each k × 10 × 20 (complete run seed × opponent generation × held-out game, 1 ≤ k ≤ 5) of one side's scores, rows the same runs in the same order; tests whether `last` is higher, paired by run, opponent generation and game, corrected for `n_comparisons` (CROSS-03, AD-23); `method` states the runs used; the test is built with the **`null-hypothesis-testing`** skill
777	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the shapes differ or k is outside 1–5
778	- **Used by:** study.run_study; PL-04
779	
780	### study.StudyReport: type
781	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class StudyReport: env: str; baselines: tuple[Comparison, ...]; ladder: tuple[Comparison, ...]; ablations: tuple[Comparison, ...]; crossplay: tuple[Comparison, ...]; spreads: dict[str, float]; incomplete: tuple[str, ...]; report: Path; charts: tuple[Path, ...]; tables: tuple[Path, ...]`
782	- **Invariants:** 2 baseline comparisons per learner and side, one ladder comparison per rung above the first in a ladder, 3 ablation comparisons, 2 cross-play comparisons per learner (Snake: 2, 0, 3, 0; tank: 6, 2, 3, 0; hunt: 12, 0, 0, 6, or 8, 0, 0, 4 without `turns`); `spreads` is each learner's (and side's) standard deviation of its per-seed mean final score (PG-10); `incomplete` names runs left out (EVAL and CROSS edge cases); a missed bar the spec calls a finding is stated as one and the study still succeeds (ABL-05, PG-11, SELF-08); `charts` is `curves.png` plus `ladder.png` in a ladder, or `curves-<side>.png` per side (CURVE-07); `tables` is one `crossplay-<rung>.md` per rung with runs, each a `TablePage.path`
783	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/study.StudyReport/ · **Cases:** a complete Snake study; a study where all 3 ablations show no detectable difference; a study with a failed ablation run; a tank study where rung 3 shows no detectable difference; a hunt study without `turns` where the simultaneous predator shows no detectable difference
784	- **Used by:** cli
785	
786	### study.run_study: The full study
787	`def run_study(env_name: str = "snake", seeds: Sequence[int] = (1, 2, 3, 4, 5), *, n_games: int = 1_000, workers: int | None = None, skip: Sequence[str] = (), root: Path = Path("runs")) -> StudyReport`
788	- **Invariants:** trains every variant of `STUDIES[env_name]` but the `skip`ped optional ones, for each seed in parallel processes, on `registry.eval_schedule`'s cadence; scores the baselines on `EVAL_SEEDS`; builds each finished run's cross-play table in the same pool when the plan has cross-play; compares on each run's `final` and tables, correcting within each family (AD-16); a skipped learner leaves every family and table (SELF-06); writes `root/<env>/study/report.md`, its charts and tables; runs unattended (QR-02)
789	- **Errors:** a failed run is reported as missing or incomplete, never as a difference: a learner's unfinished runs are left out of its baseline and cross-play comparisons, which use its finished ones; an ablation, or a rung in a ladder comparison, with any unfinished run is `missing` (AD-09); `ValueError` naming the learner if `skip` names one that isn't optional; `RuntimeError` naming the runs only if `full` has no finished run
790	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/study.run_study/ · **Cases:** a 2-seed, 100-game corridor study with one ablation run failing; the same study with one full-agent run failing; a 2-seed, 20-game line study of the 3 rungs; a 2-seed, 20-game chase study of the 3 self-play rungs with `turns` skipped
791	- **Used by:** cli; PL-04
792	
793	### study.NotesCheck: type
794	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class NotesCheck: page: Path; exists: bool; missing_sections: tuple[str, ...]; empty_sections: tuple[str, ...]; broken_links: tuple[str, ...]; ok: bool`
795	- **Invariants:** required sections are the plan's `sections`, so a page checked before any study still gets a section check: "Why it learns" (EXPL-01), one per ablation under its variant name (EXPL-02), one per ladder rung after the first under its rung name (EXPL-04), and the hunt's "Why self-play works" and one per rung (EXPL-05), an optional rung's only once it has runs; `broken_links` names each of `runs/<env>/study/report.md`, its charts and its tables that the page doesn't link or whose link doesn't resolve from the page's folder (EXPL-03); when `exists` is false every required section is missing; `ok` is true exactly when the page exists and all three tuples are empty
796	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/study.NotesCheck/ · **Cases:** a complete Snake page; a missing page; a page with an empty "Why it learns" section and no chart link; a tank page missing its `ppo` section; a hunt page missing its "Why self-play works" section and a cross-play table link
797	- **Used by:** cli; PL-06
798	
799	### study.check_notes: Check the learner's notes page
800	`def check_notes(env_name: str, *, notes_root: Path, runs_root: Path) -> NotesCheck`
801	- **Invariants:** reads `notes_root/<env>.md` and `runs_root/<env>/study/report.md` only; a section is a Markdown heading of any level, matched case-insensitively, and is empty when no non-blank text comes before the next heading of the same or a higher level; never writes, creates or changes a file, so the page is byte-identical afterwards (AD-10)

### Predator-prey tank (`HUNT`)
**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 3
**Stage:** S3
**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
**Behavior:** The fish tank with stakes. The bouncing ball is replaced by one predator fish, and food pellets are scattered around the tank. Prey lose energy every step and must eat pellets to keep it up, so a prey that only hides starves. A prey caught by the predator, or out of energy, is removed; the game ends when every prey is gone or the time limit is reached. Prey earn a reward for every step they stay alive; the predator earns one for every prey it catches. Both sides see only nearby things through rays. Each step runs in this order: every fish turns and swims, the predator catches, prey eat, then every prey loses energy.

**Requirements**
- **HUNT-01** The tank is a circle of radius 10 units holding one predator, a set number of prey from 1 to 10 (5 unless set otherwise) and 10 food pellets. The predator starts at the centre with a random heading; each prey starts at a random spot at least 3 units from the predator, with a random heading; each pellet starts at a random spot.
  - Given 5 prey and seed 2, when a game starts, then 1 predator, 5 prey and 10 pellets are inside the tank, and each prey is at least 3 units from the predator.
- **HUNT-02** Each step every living prey chooses a turn of −15° to +15° and a speed of 0 to 0.3 units per step, and the predator chooses a turn of −10° to +10° and a speed of 0 to 0.35 units per step; each fish turns first, then swims forward, and stops at the wall if it swims into it.
  - Given the predator at the centre heading east, when it chooses turn 0° and speed 0.35, then it ends 0.35 units east of the centre, still heading east.
  - Given a prey 0.1 units from the wall heading straight at it, when it swims at speed 0.3, then it ends touching the wall, inside the tank, with its heading unchanged.
- **HUNT-03** No fish's move changes another fish's position: prey pass through each other and through the predator.
  - Given two prey on the same spot, when both move, then each ends exactly where its own move would take it if it were alone.
- **HUNT-04** The predator (radius 0.5) catches every prey whose centre is closer than 0.8 units to its own (predator radius 0.5 plus prey radius 0.3) after the step's moves; a caught prey is removed.
  - Given a prey whose centre is 0.7 units from the predator's after a step, when the step is reported, then that prey is removed and is no longer asked for moves.
- **HUNT-05** Each prey starts with energy 1.0 and loses 0.002 at the end of every step; a prey whose energy reaches 0 starves and is removed.
  - Given a prey that never eats, when step 500 is reported, then its energy is 0 and it is removed.
- **HUNT-06** A prey whose centre comes within 0.5 units of a pellet (pellet radius 0.2 plus prey radius 0.3) eats it: its energy rises by 0.3, up to at most 1.0, the pellet disappears and a new pellet appears at a random spot, so 10 pellets are always in the tank.
  - Given a prey with energy 0.5 that ends its move 0.4 units from a pellet, when the step is reported, then its energy is 0.798 (0.5 + 0.3 − 0.002), that pellet is gone and 10 pellets are in the tank.
  - Given a prey with energy 0.9 that eats a pellet, when the step is reported, then its energy is 0.998 (capped at 1.0, then less 0.002).
- **HUNT-07** A game ends when every prey is gone or after 1,000 steps, with every prey still alive counted as surviving all 1,000.
  - Given 2 prey alive at step 1,000, when the step is reported, then the game has ended and both prey survived 1,000 steps.
- **HUNT-08** Each prey sees through 8 rays spread evenly around its heading, 45° apart, each reaching 4 units. Each ray reports the distance to the wall, to the predator, to the nearest other prey and to the nearest pellet along it, each scaled from 0 (touching) to 1 (nothing within reach); the prey also senses its own current speed, scaled 0 to 1, and its own energy. A prey's observation is these 34 numbers.
  - Given a prey heading east with energy 0.6, a pellet's edge 2 units straight ahead and nothing else within 4 units, when it observes, then the forward ray's pellet reading is 0.5, its energy reading is 0.6, and every other wall, predator, prey and pellet reading is 1.
- **HUNT-09** The predator sees through 8 rays spread the same way, each reaching 4 units, each reporting the distance to the wall and to the nearest prey along it, scaled from 0 to 1; it also senses its own current speed, scaled 0 to 1. The predator's observation is these 17 numbers.
  - Given the predator heading east with a prey's edge 2 units straight ahead and nothing else within 4 units, when it observes, then the forward ray's prey reading is 0.5 and every other wall and prey reading is 1.
- **HUNT-10** Each prey earns +1 for every step it survives and 0 on the step it is removed; the prey's score for a game is the average number of steps its prey survived.
  - Given 2 prey where one is caught at step 100 and the other survives to step 1,000, when the game ends, then the prey's score is 550.
- **HUNT-11** The predator earns +1 on each step for every prey it catches on that step, and 0 otherwise; the predator's score for a game is the number of prey it caught.
  - Given a step on which the predator catches 2 prey, when the step is reported, then its reward is +2.
  - Given 5 prey where the predator catches 3 and 2 starve, when the game ends, then the predator's score is 3.
- **HUNT-12** A game started with the same seed and played with the same moves unfolds identically, including every predator, prey and pellet position.
  - Given seed 7 and a fixed list of moves for both sides, when the game is played twice, then every frame, reward and both final scores are identical.

**Edge cases & errors**
- A move outside a control's limits → the step is refused with an error naming the side, the control and its limits; the game is unchanged.
- A move is sent for a removed prey, or a living prey or the predator has no move → the step is refused with an error naming the fish.
- A step is requested after the game has ended → refused with an error saying the game is over.
- A prey is caught on the step it would eat a pellet or starve → it is caught: the predator earns +1 and the pellet stays.
- Two prey reach the same pellet on the same step → the prey whose centre is closer eats it; on an exact tie, the one listed first; the other gains nothing.
- A new pellet would appear within 0.5 units of a living prey → it is placed elsewhere, so no pellet is eaten on the step it appears.
- A prey is removed on the step the time limit is reached → it is removed first, so it survived 999 steps.

### Shared core (`CORE`)
**Serves:** Goal 4
**Stage:** S1
**Behavior:** Every environment offers the same small set of behaviors: start a game from a seed, take one move for each of its acting agents and report what happened to each, say how big an observation is and what moves are allowed, and draw one frame for the replay viewer. The training loop, logging, learning curves and replay viewer use only these behaviors, so a new environment plugs in without changing them, whether its moves are a few choices or continuous controls, whether it has one agent or several, and whether one side learns or two sides learn against each other. Growing the core leaves Snake and the fish tank running exactly as before.

**Requirements**
- **CORE-01** Every environment can start a new game from a given seed and returns the first observation.
  - Given any environment and seed 3, when a game is started twice, then both first observations are identical.
- **CORE-02** Every environment takes one move for each of its acting agents (Snake: the snake; the tank: each living fish; the predator-prey tank: the predator and each living prey) and reports, for each, its next observation and reward, plus whether the game has ended.
  - Given a running Snake game, when a move is taken, then one next observation, one reward and the game-over flag are reported.
  - Given a tank game with 3 living fish, when 3 moves are taken, then 3 next observations, 3 rewards and the game-over flag are reported.
  - Given a predator-prey game with 4 living prey, when 5 moves are taken (the predator's and 4 prey's), then 5 next observations, 5 rewards and the game-over flag are reported.
- **CORE-03** Every environment reports, for each of its sides, its observation size and its allowed moves: either a number of choices, or a number of continuous controls with each control's limits.
  - Given Snake, when asked, then it reports 11 facts and 3 choices.
  - Given the tank, when asked, then it reports 25 numbers and 2 controls: turn −15° to +15°, speed 0 to 0.3.
  - Given the predator-prey tank, when asked, then it reports two sides: the prey with 34 numbers and 2 controls (turn −15° to +15°, speed 0 to 0.3), and the predator with 17 numbers and 2 controls (turn −10° to +10°, speed 0 to 0.35).
- **CORE-04** Every environment draws a frame of its current state for the replay viewer.
  - Given a running Snake game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the grid, the snake, the food, the score and the step number.
  - Given a running tank game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the tank, the ball, each living fish with its heading, the number of fish left and the step number.
  - Given a running predator-prey game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the tank, the pellets, the predator with its heading, each living prey with its heading and energy, the number of prey left and the step number.
- **CORE-05** The training loop trains any agent on any environment that offers CORE-01 to CORE-04, for a set number of games, with a given seed.
  - Given a tiny test environment (a 1 × 5 corridor where reaching the right end gives +1), when the training loop runs on it with no changes to the core, then training completes and the agent reaches the right end in ≥ 9 of 10 evaluation games.
- **CORE-06** Logging, learning curves and the replay viewer work on any environment that offers CORE-01 to CORE-04.
  - Given the tiny test environment, when a run is logged, its learning curve drawn and a game replayed, then each works with no changes to the core.
- **CORE-07** The training loop, logging, learning curves and replay viewer work on an environment with continuous controls and several agents at once.
  - Given a tiny continuous test environment (2 points on a line from 0 to 5, each pushing itself −1 to +1 per step, +1 for reaching 5), when a policy-gradient agent trains on it with no changes to the core, then training completes, both points reach 5 in ≥ 9 of 10 evaluation games, and the run is logged, drawn and replayed.
- **CORE-08** When an environment removes one of its agents mid-game, the training loop stops asking that agent for moves and the game continues for the rest.
  - Given the continuous test environment where one point reaches 5 first, when the next step is taken, then only the other point is asked for a move.
- **CORE-09** Growing the core leaves Snake's and the fish tank's results unchanged.
  - Given a 200-game Snake run with seed 1 recorded before the core grew, when it is repeated after, then every logged evaluation score is identical.
  - Given a 200-game rung-3 tank run with seed 1 recorded before the core grew, when it is repeated after, then every logged evaluation score is identical.
- **CORE-10** The training loop trains an environment with two sides, each with its own agent, and each side either learns or plays frozen, without learning, in any game.
  - Given a tiny two-sided test environment (a chaser and a runner on a line from 0 to 5, each pushing itself −1 to +1 per step; the chaser earns +1 for touching the runner, the runner +1 for every step untouched), when both sides train with no changes to the core, then training completes, both sides' policies change, and the run is logged, drawn and replayed.
  - Given the same environment with the runner frozen, when a training game ends, then the chaser's policy is updated and the runner's is identical to before the game.

**Edge cases & errors**
- An environment is missing one of the 4 behaviors → the core refuses it at start-up and names the missing behavior.
- An agent returns a move outside the environment's allowed choices or limits → the run stops with an error naming the move and the game step.
- An agent built for choices is given an environment with continuous controls, or the other way round → refused at start-up, naming both kinds.
- A two-sided environment is started with an agent for only one side → refused at start-up, naming the side with no agent.

### Self-play ladder (`SELF`)
**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
**Stage:** S3
**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
**Behavior:** In the predator-prey tank both sides learn, each with its own copy of the limited-update agent, so each side's world keeps changing as the other improves. A ladder of three ways to train them shows why: taking turns (optional) keeps each learner's world still; simultaneous learners, the broken version kept on purpose, chase a moving target; self-play against past versions, the fix, trains each side against a mix of its opponent's current and saved past generations. Every run saves generations of both sides as it goes, for the cross-play table.

**Requirements**
- **SELF-01** Both sides are trained by the limited-update agent (PG-08), each side with its own policy: one shared by all prey, and one for the predator.
  - Given a predator-prey training run, when it starts, then two policies exist, one per side, and each learns only from its own side's steps.
- **SELF-02** Every predator-prey run saves a generation of each side at every tenth of training, 10 generations per side, the last at the end of training.
  - Given a finished run, when its saved generations are listed, then there are 10 predator generations and 10 prey generations, numbered 1 to 10 in the order they were saved.
- **SELF-03** In the taking-turns rung, training is split into 10 equal blocks that alternate sides, prey first; in each block one side learns while the other plays its latest policy frozen.
  - Given a taking-turns run, when block 1 ends, then the prey's policy has changed and the predator's is identical to its starting policy.
  - Given block 2, when it ends, then the predator's policy has changed and the prey's is identical to its policy at the end of block 1.
- **SELF-04** In the simultaneous rung, every training game pits the current predator against the current prey, and both sides learn after every game.
  - Given a simultaneous run, when any training game ends, then both sides' policies are updated from that game.
- **SELF-05** In the self-play rung, each training game is drawn as one of three kinds: current against current, both learning (50%); current predator against a past prey generation, only the predator learning (25%); a past predator generation against current prey, only the prey learning (25%). The past generation is drawn uniformly from the opponent's generations saved so far; until the first is saved, every game is current against current.
  - Given 10,000 self-play training games after the first generations are saved, when their kinds are counted, then 48–52% are current against current and 23–27% are each of the other two kinds.
  - Given 4 saved prey generations and 10,000 games against past prey, when the drawn generations are counted, then each was drawn in 23–27% of them.
  - Given a game of the current predator against a past prey generation, when it ends, then the predator's policy is updated and neither the current prey policy nor the saved generation changes.
- **SELF-06** The taking-turns rung is optional: S3 is complete with only the simultaneous and self-play rungs.
  - Given finished studies of the simultaneous and self-play rungs and no taking-turns runs, when the stage is checked, then it is complete, and every comparison and table leaves out the missing rung without counting it as failed.
- **SELF-07** A saved generation of either side can be loaded back, and the loaded generation picks the same moves.
  - Given a saved predator generation, when it is loaded and plays an evaluation game with seed 11 against the scripted prey, then it makes exactly the same moves as before saving.
- **SELF-08** A taking-turns or simultaneous rung that does not beat both baselines (EVAL-03), or whose later generations do not beat its earlier ones (CROSS-03), is recorded as a finding in the notes, and S3 doesn't count as failed.
  - Given a simultaneous rung whose last predator generation shows "no detectable difference" from its first, when the stage is checked, then it passes, and the notes page records it as a finding (EXPL-05).

**Edge cases & errors**
- A game where every prey is removed on the first step → both learning sides learn from those steps alone; the run does not stop.
- A saved generation is loaded for the other side → loading is refused with an error naming both sides' observation sizes.
- A run stops before all 10 generations are saved → the saved ones are kept and the run is marked incomplete.

### Cross-play table (`CROSS`)
**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
**Stage:** S3
**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
**Behavior:** Each side's reward alone can't show learning, because its opponent improves too. The cross-play table pits every saved generation of the predator against every saved generation of the prey on the same held-out games, so the learner can see whether later generations beat earlier ones, which is real progress, or whether the two sides only chase each other in circles.

**Requirements**
- **CROSS-01** For each finished predator-prey run, every predator generation plays every prey generation on the same 20 held-out games with exploration off, filling a 10 × 10 table of the predator's mean score and the prey's mean score for each pairing.
  - Given a finished run with 10 generations per side, when its table is built, then it has 100 cells, each holding both sides' mean scores over the same 20 held-out games.
- **CROSS-02** The table is shown with predator generations as rows and prey generations as columns, one table per rung averaged over its 5 training seeds, built from saved generations only, never by training again.
  - Given the 5 seeds of the self-play rung, when its table is shown, then each cell holds both sides' mean scores over the 5 seeds' tables.
- **CROSS-03** For each rung and each side, the last generation's scores are compared with the first generation's with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, given each generation's scores against all 10 opponent generations on the same 20 held-out games for each of the 5 training seeds; the later generation beats the earlier only if its scores are higher at a 5% significance level after correcting for every such comparison in the predator-prey tank (6: 3 rungs × 2 sides; 4 without the taking-turns rung).
  - Given finished runs of every rung, when the comparison runs, then the report says, for each rung's predator and each rung's prey, "later beats earlier" or "no detectable difference", with the test result.
- **CROSS-04** In the self-play rung, the last generation beats the first for both the predator and the prey.
  - Given the CROSS-03 report, when it is read, then the self-play rung's predator and prey each show "later beats earlier".

**Edge cases & errors**
- A run has fewer than 10 generations saved → its table covers the saved ones, is marked incomplete, and the run is left out of the CROSS-03 comparison, which names it.

### Baselines (`BASE`)
**Serves:** Goal 2
**Stage:** S1
**Behavior:** Two non-learning agents play the same seeded games as the learning agents in each environment: a random agent and a simple scripted agent. In the predator-prey tank each side has both, a random and a scripted predator and a random and a scripted prey. Their scores set the bars every learning agent's curve must clear.

**Requirements**
- **BASE-01** The random agent picks each move uniformly at random from the allowed moves: one of the choices, or each control anywhere within its limits.
  - Given 10,000 moves by the random agent on Snake, when counted, then each of the 3 moves is picked 30–37% of the time.
  - Given 10,000 moves by the random agent in the tank, when counted, then turns are spread evenly over −15° to +15° and speeds over 0 to 0.3, each tenth of either range holding 9–11% of moves.
- **BASE-02** On Snake, the scripted agent moves toward the food along the axis with the larger gap, and when that move is immediately fatal it takes any non-fatal move, preferring straight.
  - Given food straight ahead and no danger ahead, when the scripted agent moves, then it goes straight.
  - Given food straight ahead and a wall ahead, when it moves, then it turns to a side that is free.
- **BASE-03** Both baselines are scored on the same 100 held-out games as the learning agent.
  - Given the 100 held-out seeds, when each baseline plays them, then each records one score per seed, and its mean and spread are logged.
- **BASE-04** In the tank, the scripted fish swims at full speed, turning as far as it can away from the ray that sees the ball closest; with no ball in reach it swims straight, and when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room.
  - Given the ball seen only on the front-left ray and no wall within 1 unit ahead, when the scripted fish moves, then it turns −15° (to the right) at speed 0.3.
  - Given no ball in reach and the wall 0.5 units straight ahead with more room on the left, when it moves, then it turns +15° at speed 0.3.

- **BASE-05** The scripted predator swims at full speed, turning as far as it can toward the ray that sees a prey closest (not turning when that ray points straight ahead); with no prey in reach it swims straight, and when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room.
  - Given a prey seen only on the front-left ray, when the scripted predator moves, then it turns +10° (to the left) at speed 0.35.
  - Given no prey in reach and the wall 0.5 units straight ahead with more room on the right, when it moves, then it turns −10° at speed 0.35.
- **BASE-06** The scripted prey swims at full speed: when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room; otherwise, when the predator is in reach it turns as far as it can away from the ray that sees the predator closest; otherwise it turns as far as it can toward the ray that sees a pellet closest; with nothing in reach it swims straight.
  - Given the predator seen only on the front-left ray and no wall within 1 unit ahead, when the scripted prey moves, then it turns −15° at speed 0.3.
  - Given no predator in reach and a pellet seen only on the right ray, when it moves, then it turns −15° at speed 0.3.

**Edge cases & errors**
- Every move is fatal → the scripted agent goes straight and the game ends.
- The scripted fish sees the ball equally close on two rays → it turns away from the one further left.
- The scripted fish sees the ball and the wall ahead at once → it avoids the wall.
- The scripted predator or prey sees its target equally close on two rays → it uses the one further left.

## Quality requirements
- **QR-01** Every training run of a learning agent finishes in under ~1 hour (≤ 60 minutes) on the learner's laptop CPU: the value-learning agent on Snake; each rung and ablation in the tank with 5 fish; and each rung in the predator-prey tank with 5 prey, where one run is the whole run, every generation of both sides.
  - Timed on the learner's laptop with no GPU, for the default number of training games.
- **QR-02** Each environment's full study runs unattended to completion with one command: Snake's (full agent and 3 ablations × 5 seeds, plus baselines) in ≤ 10 hours; the tank's (3 rungs and 3 ablations × 5 seeds, plus baselines) in ≤ 15 hours; the predator-prey tank's (3 rungs × 5 seeds, plus both sides' baselines and every run's cross-play table) in ≤ 16 hours, or ≤ 11 hours without the taking-turns rung.
  - Started once on the learner's laptop, it finishes without intervention; total time read from the run records.
- **QR-03** Every learning algorithm is written by hand: the value targets, remembered steps, frozen copy, exploration and update step of the value-learning agent, the move spreads, returns, value estimate, advantages, update cap and update step of the ladder, and the generation saving, frozen side and opponent mix of the self-play ladder; no reinforcement-learning library is used. General array math with automatic gradients is allowed.
  - Checked by reading the project's dependency list and the agents' code.
- **QR-04** The lab uses only free tools and needs no paid service or account.
  - Checked by reading the dependency list: every item is free to use.
- **QR-05** On the same machine, a training run repeated with the same seed and settings produces the same logged results.
  - Two runs of 200 games with seed 1 give identical evaluation scores.

## Assumptions
- The learner is the only user; the lab runs on their laptop.
- Tiny test environments exist only to prove the shared core; they are not lab environments.
- Snake: 20 × 20 grid, starting length 3, step limit 100 × length, rewards +10 / −10 / 0.
- Value-learning agent settings: exploration 1.0 → 0.01 over half the games, memory of 100,000 steps, discount 0.9, frozen copy refreshed every 1,000 steps, fixed 0.1 exploration when decay is off.
- Study size: 5 training seeds per agent, 100 held-out games; Snake evaluates every 50 games, the tank at every tenth of training on 20 games plus a final 100; Snake's study ≤ 10 hours.
- The scripted baseline avoids immediately fatal moves; the agent may only tie it, and that bar is kept on purpose.
- Tank: radius 10, ball radius 1 at 0.25 per step, fish radius 0.3, 5 fish by default (1–10), 1,000-step limit; turn −15° to +15° then swim 0 to 0.3 per step.
- A fish senses 8 rays of reach 4 (wall, ball, nearest other fish) plus its own speed, and doesn't sense the ball's motion; the bet is that the rays are enough to dodge.
- Tank reward is +1 per step alive, with no extra penalty when hit; the scripted fish flees the ball and steers clear of the wall, a tougher bar kept on purpose.
- Ladder settings: discount 0.99, blend 0.95, one update per game, 10 passes in batches of 64, a 20% cap; the tank's switches apply to rung 3.
- Learning "more steadily" is reported as the spread across seeds, not tested; the tank's study is ≤ 15 hours.
- The discrete tank is optional; S2 is complete without it.
- Predator-prey tank: radius 10, 5 prey by default (1–10) moving as S2's fish, one predator of radius 0.5 turning −10° to +10° and swimming 0 to 0.35 per step (faster but less agile), catching at < 0.8; 1,000-step limit. Only prey have energy: 1.0 at the start, −0.002 per step, +0.3 per pellet up to 1.0. 10 pellets of radius 0.2 are always present, and a contested pellet goes to the closer prey.
- Prey see 34 numbers (wall, predator, nearest other prey and nearest pellet on 8 rays of reach 4, plus speed and energy); the predator sees 17 (wall and nearest prey on 8 rays, plus speed). Prey earn +1 per step alive; the predator +1 per catch.
- Self-play settings: 10 generations per side per run, one at every tenth of training; games are 50% current against current, 25% against a past prey generation and 25% against a past predator generation, each past one drawn uniformly. Taking turns is 10 alternating blocks, prey first, and is optional.
- Only the self-play rung must beat both baselines of each side and show later generations beating earlier ones; the other rungs are reported. The cross-play table is 10 × 10 over 20 held-out games; the predator-prey study is ≤ 16 hours, or ≤ 11 without taking turns.

# Thickening, routing, modes, checkpoints, the graph, writing

Read when writing thicken tasks, the dependency graph and the files. Templates: `../examples.md`; graph example: `examples-phase0.md`; routing example: `examples-routing.md`.

## 5. Thicken

`**Kind:** thicken`, under Phase 2: one task per step upgrade, depending on its step's spine. Order in the file sets nothing: every thicken task can start once its spine lands. Risk orders tasks only when a later one must learn from an earlier one, as an `order` edge with its reason (step 9). In data and ML work, data loading, leak checks and the evaluation get thicken tasks of their own, never left until after the model.

**Split a task** when it needs more than one session, has more than 3 acceptance bullets, touches two packages or two steps, or has "and" in its title. Sizes count `Writes:` entries outside `tests/` (`task-size`; a folder glob counts as one): XS = 1, S = 1–2, M = 3–5. Nothing larger. Spine and thicken tasks are XS or S.

**Acceptance is test-backed.** Each bullet is checkable: a `TC-NN` passes, a Given/When/Then, a model criterion by ID, the smoke command. With no `tests.md`, write the criterion so a tester can encode it. A TC that runs the whole pipeline is the wiring or system task's acceptance (`phase0.md`), never a thicken's. A thicken's golden case builds its store from its own fixture (`input.json`) through a shared test helper, never through a wiring task's run, so no thicken waits on wiring.

## 6. Route skills and references

**Skills.** Start from `milestone_skills` (the milestone's `**Skills:**` line, else its stage's): each goes on the `**Skills:**` of every task it fits, and at least one. Then add others whose descriptions fit a task (project `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `skills_available`). The plan's Scope lists the milestone's skills. A task that needs a skill that isn't installed names it anyway (`dt status` reports it under `skills_missing`) and goes under Open questions. Never quietly plan the step without it.

**References.** Each `RF-NN` or `PB-NN` in `milestone_references` goes in the `Read:` of the tasks whose step it informs; an RF entry's *Use for* and *Key parts* say which, a PB entry's *Informs* and *Caveats*. Cite the ID, never the `references/` or `probes/` path. A probe's code goes to the step that calls the same API (`PB-01:probe.py:sync_all`); its redacted samples (`PB-01:samples`) go to the interface task (`phase0.md`) and, as raw-shaped test input, to that step's spine and thicken tasks. Over 300 lines, add a selector so the packet stays small: `RF-01:deseasonalize` (one function or class), `RF-01:outline`, or `RF-03:<heading>` for a doc. With no selector a long file prints as its outline and `dt lint` warns `ref-large`. Whether to reuse the code is the builder's call; don't write it into Acceptance. A decision whose Consequences adopt what a probe spent (Plaid Items it linked) gets its own task in the milestone that builds that step.

## 7. Build mode

- `split` (tester, then implementer) when the task adds or changes behavior with a checkable outcome: anything a TC case, Given/When/Then or model criterion covers.
- `solo` for interface, spine, wiring, system, refactor, glue and doc-sync tasks.

`mode_violations` flags a task that breaks the rule. An override needs `**Reason:** <one line>` on the task. **A split task tests symbols a frozen interface declares** (the interface task's types and signatures), so the tester never writes against a guess and starts right after the interface, beside the implementers. Its `Depends on` is the implementer half's; the tester half waits only on the interface.

## 8. Checkpoints

`### Checkpoint after M<n>-T<nn>` at the end of a wave, naming the wave's last task in plan order: `### Checkpoint after M<n>-T<nn> (barrier)` after the interface task(s), so nothing builds on an unreviewed interface, and usually once after the wiring and thicken wave, when every pipeline runs end to end. A plan without the barrier still gets the review: `dt` adds the barrier. `### Checkpoint: milestone exit` last. Use the blocks in `../examples.md`. A separate read-only reviewer runs each one; the orchestrator records its findings with `dt finding`, not this skill.

## 9. Dependencies, Writes, the graph, Verify

- **`Depends on:`** resolves, points only backward (position counts, not ID number), is acyclic, and holds only edges nothing mitigates.
- **`Writes:`** is exact paths, or a folder glob the task owns, and is an exclusive claim: two tasks with no `Depends on` path between them never write the same path (`writes`). Shared files (`__init__.py`, `types.py`, `__main__.py`, and shared test helpers: `conftest.py`, a golden-case store builder) belong to the interface task; each pipeline's entry module to its wiring and system tasks (`phase0.md`). No `Writes:` under `tasks/`: builders never tick `todo.md` or record findings; the orchestrator does after each merge.
- **`## Dependency graph`**, after the last checkpoint: one row per edge between tasks, `| From | To | Kind | Mitigated by |`. From is the task that needs, To the one it needs; each cell starts with the task ID. Mitigated by names the interface and the task freezing it (`profile.Profile sample (M1-T01)`), or says `none: <reason>`.

| Kind | Meaning | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| `data` | One step's output feeds another's input | The type and its sample |
| `call` | A step calls a function another task builds | Across pipelines or packages, its fixture-backed fake; in the caller's own pipeline and package, its replay double (`phase0.md` a), never a fake |
| `file` | Two tasks must write one file | Move the shared part to the interface task; else serial. Two wiring tasks never share a file: each writes its own entry module. Two steps' thicken tasks on one module: the interface task's layout splits it (each step its own file, the old module re-exporting); never chain them |
| `code` | A task changes code another task wrote (a thicken after its step's spine or an earlier thicken of that step) | None: serial |
| `contract-change` | A task changes a frozen interface | None: serial, plus a new interface task |
| `order` | Integration (a wiring or system task runs the steps it wires: `none: integration: the smoke runs it`), or risk or learning ("do X first to learn Y"; never a human approval: `phase0.md` a) | None: reason required |

A spine never has an edge to another step's task: it builds against the interface's samples and doubles. A mitigated edge documents why two tasks run side by side and never appears in `Depends on`; an unmitigated one must (`edges`). Every `Depends on` has its row, except a dependency on the interface task, which every task has and needs none. The table is the reviewable argument for every serial step.
- **Width.** `dt status` gives `critical_path`, `waves`, `width` and `parallelism`; on the fresh plan `dt ready` lists only the interface task(s). Mostly width-1 waves mean an edge an interface could mitigate is ordering tasks.
- **`Verify:`** The interface task's runs the schema check over every sample and each fake's contract cases; every other task's runs its focused tests and `scripts/check.sh gate` (`--pkg <pkg>` in multi-package), plus its pipeline's smoke command (tests.md Strategy, else the Trigger plus ` --smoke`) once its `Depends on` path reaches that pipeline's wiring task: wiring and system tasks always. A thicken task running beside the wiring is smoke-tested by the gate after each merge. Only the modes `fast`, `gate` and `full` exist.
- **`Read:`** lists the IDs (references as `RF-NN[:selector]`, probes as `PB-NN:<file>[:<symbol>]`, `PB-NN:outline` or `PB-NN:samples`) and paths a builder needs, and nothing else. A split task's names its step's function, so the tester knows the signature it tests. Check each packet with `dt context <task>`: it must resolve and stay under 2,000 lines.

## 10. Write

Task IDs start at `dt next-id M<n>-T` and count up, in phase order.
- **Next milestone only.** Later milestones stay as roadmap outlines. Write `tasks/queued/M<n>-<slug>.md` only when the user asks.
- `tasks/plan.md` with Status `Draft` and `**Base:** —` (pending). Never record a sha yourself: `build-setup` runs `dt base` after its bar is approved and committed.
- `tasks/todo.md`: one unchecked line per task, IDs matching the plan.
- Milestone rows and sections (`cut.md` step 2), the planned milestone's `**Exit:**` and `**Plan:**`, then `dt roadmap`.
38:**Frozen means frozen.** Changing a frozen type, sample, signature or fake is a `contract-change`: `architecture-design` Revise, then a new interface task naming the symbol in `Read:`, never an edit to make a test pass. The bar enforces it (CR-09): once a contract folder exists at a task's base the implementer can't write it, and `dt guard` asks before any edit no open interface task covers. The re-freeze runs in the main session, since the implementer's guard blocks a folder frozen at the base.
